It would NOT be enough to prove improvement\nfrom an upper certificate W<=1/D: that would need an UPPER bound for B_n\nat the actual physical horizon.\n\nThe unconditional parts of accepted 04219554e41d9750 supply monotonicity,\nB_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n, and for every positive integer b the finite\npositive ray Z_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), with\n\n lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1)<=Z_b\n      <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2.                (4)\n\nIts adjacent estimate is B_(n+1)-B_n<=8sqrt(2)(n+1)^(rho-1).\nConsequently shifting a ray index by a FIXED finite number changes the\nlambda^(-j)-scaled value by a quantity tending to zero: sum the finite\nnumber of adjacent bounds and divide by lambda^j=2^(rho j). This also\ntreats the positive offsets used below. In particular the physical\nbenchmark limits for our three layouts are respectively Z_1, Z_3,\nand Z_2=lambda Z_1 after scaling by C. These are unconditional benchmark\nstatements; no seed hypothesis is assumed. No scalar 237/250 from a\ndifferent word is used anywhere in this report.\n\n## 3. Exact accepted legal witnesses, with every original jet retained\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for ANY nonnegative physical word w,\n\n W_N(w)>=1/(1+2sum w),\n W_N(w)>=(w_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)],                  (5)\n\nwhere P_j=sum_(t<j)w_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)w_t, and (z)_+=max(z,0).\nThus these witnesses, not unverified copies of them, are available as\nbricks for the joint screen.\n\nTo make their application and the all-function audit concrete, here are\ntheir exact functions and all physical queried data. No external theorem is imported.\n\n## 1. Original problem, normalization, and exact lower blocks\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of physical length N, use\n\n W_N(h)=sup 2(f(x_N)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),\n x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t).\n\nThe supremum ranges over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex\ndifferentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every\nminimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star. The whole word is selected before\nf, x_0, or any oracle observations. All actual-function witnesses below\nuse dimension one, x_0=1, the UNIQUE minimizer x_star=0, and normalized\nsmoothness L=1. Formal matrix test vectors are separately identified.\nThus the true closest initial squared radius is exactly 1. Multiplying\nthe function by any L>0 preserves the iterates under h_t/L and preserves\nthe displayed normalized payoff. There is ONE static function per full\nphysical run, including every lower block and every upper position.\n\nWrite lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda). The following word is called\nq_m locally in this report; it is not the parked perturbed ruler word:\n\n q_0=(),\n q_(m+1)=(q_m, 1+(lambda-2)*lambda^m, q_m).\n\nPut s_m=2^m-1 and C=lambda^m. Here C is ONLY the local lower-block\ncapacity, distinct from the benchmark C_n below. The exact physical\nlower word has length s_m, sum C-1, and a full primitive certificate with\nZERO remainder on all s_m+1 formal gradient coordinates. No lower\ncoefficient is perturbed.\n\nHere is an explicit definition of every coefficient in that lower\ncertificate, sufficient without evaluating any recurrence. For any word\nw of length r, keep every formal g_0,...,g_r and value f_0,...,f_r, and set\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)w_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_w=2sum_(t<r)w_t(f_t-f_r)+sum_(t<r)w_t(1-w_t)||g_t||^2\n                                      -(sum w)(1+sum w)||g_r||^2.\n\nFor a zero-diagonal array Lambda, let e be the all-ones column,\nL_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1, T_it=w_t for t<i, zero for t>i,\nomega=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(r-1),2sum w)^T, and\n\n D_prim=diag(w_t(2-w_t) for t<r, -(sum w)(2+sum w)).\n\nIn a full primitive certificate the required flow condition is the\nfirst line below. Under that condition, accepted 04219554e41d9750 gives\nthe exact identity on ALL original coordinates in the second line:\n\n L_flow^T e=omega,\n V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>,\n M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T).                                (1)\n\nHere sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2, Gram is the full matrix of inner products, and\nthe matrix pairing is trace(M_prim^T Gram). Start q_0 with array zero.\nAt the balanced join from q_m to q_(m+1), set\nd=(lambda-2)C, kappa=lambda^2, let p be the bridge query and z the\nparent terminal, and let j range over EVERY right-child nonterminal\nquery with its actual step w_j. Embed the complete left array with\nfactor 1, the complete right array with factor kappa, and add\n\n Lambda_(j,p) += (kappa-1)w_j,\n Lambda_(z,p) += kappa-1,\n Lambda_(p,z) += (kappa-1)d,\n Lambda_(j,z) += (kappa-1)w_j.                               (2)\n\nCoincident contributions add. Every unlisted entry is zero. All signs\nare nonnegative, since d>0 and kappa>1. The defining root equation is\nd(2C+d)=C^2, and the parent capacity is 2C+d=lambda C. Accepted 042's\nfull primitive join identity gives M_parent=M_left+kappa M_right on\ntheir original embedded coordinates. Induction starting with M=0 proves\nthe asserted ZERO full remainder for q_m. Every internal original value\nis cancelled by its exact flow in (1); every internal gradient remains\nin that exact identity. A block is never declared to be one oracle step.\n\nFor later calibration, on the actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 the\nendpoint factor of q_m is -1/C for m>=1. Indeed the factor at the next\nbalanced join is -(lambda-2)C times the square of the child factor,\nand lambda-2=1/lambda. This is a symbolic induction, not an executed run.\n\n## 2. Original benchmark and orientation of comparisons\n\nUse exactly the original all-split sequences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                             (3)\n\nIn particular all original integer split indices remain available. A\nparticular legal split gives a LOWER bound for B_n. Such a lower bound\nis enough to disprove a proposed improving word when an actual witness\nhas B_n times its payoff >1. It would NOT be enough to prove improvement\nfrom an upper certificate W<=1/D: that would need an UPPER bound for B_n\nat the actual physical horizon.\n\nThe unconditional parts of accepted 04219554e41d9750 supply monotonicity,\nB_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n, and for every positive integer b the finite\npositive ray Z_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), with\n\n lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1)<=Z_b\n      <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2.                (4)\n\nIts adjacent estimate is B_(n+1)-B_n<=8sqrt(2)(n+1)^(rho-1).\nConsequently shifting a ray index by a FIXED finite number changes the\nlambda^(-j)-scaled value by a quantity tending to zero: sum the finite\nnumber of adjacent bounds and divide by lambda^j=2^(rho j). This also\ntreats the positive offsets used below. In particular the physical\nbenchmark limits for our three layouts are respectively Z_1, Z_3,\nand Z_2=lambda Z_1 after scaling by C. These are unconditional benchmark\nstatements; no seed hypothesis is assumed. No scalar 237/250 from a\ndifferent word is used anywhere in this report.\n\n## 3. Exact accepted legal witnesses, with every original jet retained\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for ANY nonnegative physical word w,\n\n W_N(w)>=1/(1+2sum w),\n W_N(w)>=(w_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)],                  (5)\n\nwhere P_j=sum_(t<j)w_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)w_t, and (z)_+=max(z,0).\nThus these witnesses, not unverified copies of them, are available as\nbricks for the joint screen.\n\nTo make their application and the all-function audit concrete, here are\ntheir exact functions and all physical queried data. For p,q>0 let\n\n F_(p,q)(x)= -q*x-q^2/2                 for x<=-q,\n              x^2/2                    for -q<=x<=p,\n              p*x-p^2/2                for x>=p.             (6)\n\nIts derivative max(-q,min(p,x)) is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz. Hence it is convex differentiable 1-smooth with unique\nminimum 0 at 0. These are precisely the asymmetric Huber functions\nin the accepted cut fact.\n\nFor a nonzero cut with b=w_j>1, put p=1/(P_j+1) and\nq=p(b-1)/(2T_j+1). Before the cut, for every original physical index t,\n\n x_t=1-p*sum_(u<t)w_u >=p,\n g_t=p, f_t=p*x_t-p^2/2.\n\nAt the cut x_j=p, and the next point is -q(2T_j+1). On its ENTIRE\nremaining physical suffix, at accumulated suffix mass v in [0,T_j],\n\n x=-q(2T_j+1)+q*v <=-q,\n g=-q, f=-q*x-q^2/2.\n\nThe terminal payoff is q^2(2T_j+1), exactly the cut term in (5). This\nspecifies every queried and terminal jet, not only the block endpoints.\nIf a cut step is <=1, its term is zero and no zero-slope witness is\nasserted to have a unique minimum. The separate positive global Huber\nwitness always remains available.\n\nFor the global term in (5), write D=1+2sum w and use (6) with p=q=1/D.\nEvery original point is x_t=1-(sum_(u<t)w_u)/D>=1/D, so its gradient is\n1/D and its value is x_t/D-1/(2D^2). At the terminal, 2f_N=1/D.\nThis includes words with zero steps and the empty word.\n\nFor each constructed whole function, accepted 1252b8823bb5a290 gives\nQ_ij<=0 between EVERY pair of original jets, in both orientations,\nincluding the true optimum and the terminal. Its full Gram is the\nouter product of the actual scalar coordinates and is PSD. No remote\nrow, initial mixed term, boundary sample, or gradient is discarded.\nEquivalently this inequality follows from the necessity proof in that\naccepted fact applied to the explicitly checked derivative of (6).\n\nThis immediately audits ANY purported full objective upper certificate\nat a level q_cert below one of these witness payoffs. After the physical\nGD substitution it would be\n\n M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_N+sum lambda_ij Q_ij,\n lambda_ij>=0,\n\nwith exact full value flow and full PSD on (x_0,g_0,...,g_N). Evaluating\nat the legal scalar transcript gives M_cert<=q_cert-2f_N<0. Thus full\nPSD is impossible at that level, irrespective of rank, support,\nnonadjacent rows, objective-only resources, or singular directions.\nThis is an actual-function rejection, not a coarse PSD test.\n\n## 6. Gadget C: two exact blocks and two arbitrary upper coefficients\n\nConsider the ENTIRE following physical layout, not just one fixed probe:\n\n a_m>=0, b_m>=0 chosen before the function,\n H_C(m;a_m,b_m)=(q_m,1+a_m C,q_m,b_m),\n N_C=2s_m+2=2^(m+1),\n D_C=(4+2a_m)C+2b_m-1.                                    (21)\n\nThere are exactly two upper positions and two exact primitive lower\nblocks. The final coefficient b_m may be zero. Every original physical\ngradient remains a coordinate, even at a zero last step. The parameters\ncan depend arbitrarily on m and on the known word, but on no function\nor oracle information. The condition on the first upper coefficient is\n1+a_m C>=1; coefficients below 1 are outside this screened layout.\n\nAn explicit top gadget in this layout is\n\n a_g=(sqrt(5)-1)/2,\n gamma=4+2a_g=3+sqrt(5),\n a_m=a_g, b_m=1+sqrt(C/2).                                 (22)\n\nIt has D_C=gamma C+1+sqrt(2C). Since a_g^2+a_g=1,\na_g^2 gamma=2. The unit quadratic on ONE full physical run has\nendpoint factor\n(-1/C)(-a_g C)(-1/C)(-sqrt(C/2))=a_g/sqrt(2C),\nso its actual payoff is exactly 1/(gamma C). This is a physical\nquadratic lower witness, not a virtual endpoint certificate.\n\n### 6.1 The complete necessary system from accepted original cuts\n\nFor arbitrary a=a_m>=0 and b=b_m>=0, applying (5) at the actual\nfirst bridge and at the actual last step gives\n\n W_(N_C)(H_C)>= max{\n   1/[(4+2a)C+2b-1],\n   a^2/[2C+2b-1],\n   (b-1)_+^2/[(2+a)^2 C^2] }.                              (23)\n\nFor the first bridge, P=C-1, its excess is aC, and its whole physical\nsuffix mass is C-1+b. For the last step, P=(2+a)C-1 and suffix mass\nzero. These are exact actual masses, including every internal step of\nthe second lower block; there is no coarse replacement. The denominators\nare positive since C>=1 and a,b>=0.\n\nFor a>0, the first cut is the actual static function\nF_(1/C,a/(2C+2b-1)). It starts the entire first block on its positive\naffine piece, the first upper step sends 1/C to -a, and all of the\nsecond block and last step stay on its negative affine piece. Their\nevery original point is given by Section 3 with suffix mass C-1+b.\nFor a=0 this necessary inequality is zero and follows without dividing\nby a. For b>1, the terminal cut uses p=1/[(2+a)C], q=p(b-1);\nall original preterminal points are on the positive affine piece and\nthe last step reaches -q. For b<=1 its necessary term is zero. Thus\nall boundary cases, including a=0 and b=0, are treated in (23).\n\nAn attempted improving full objective certificate under accepted\n1252 would have outgoing terminal probabilities alpha_j>=0 summing\nto 1, all queried-prefix multipliers nonnegative with flow 2alpha_i,\nand full remainder\n\n M_cert=||g_N-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha,\n V_alpha=x_N-sum_j alpha_j(x_j-g_j),\n R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_j alpha_j(f_j-||g_j||^2/2)\n             -||V_alpha||^2+sum lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.         (24)\n\nThe optimum jet is included with x_star=g_star=f_star=0. This is the\nfull original prefix Schur identity; it deletes no prefix rows or\ninternal gradients. At ANY level contradicted by (23), the legal\nwitness evaluation of Section 3 makes M_cert negative. Thus no choice\nof original nonadjacent rows, simplex weights, initial mixed terms or\nfull PSD remainder can repair that improving level. The exact lower\nidentities remain available, but no endpoint-oracle substitution can\noverrule those original transcripts.\n\n### 6.2 A strict finite all-split benchmark comparison, done textually\n\nDefine eta=29/21000. We now derive, jointly, the useful strict lower\nbenchmark comparison\n\n B_(N_C)/C > gamma+eta,  m>=2.                             (25)\n\nThis does not evaluate the benchmark recurrence. It displays legal\nsplits and exact elementary inequalities. In (3), C_1=lambda,\nC_2=Omega(1,lambda)>4, and the split (1,2) gives\nC_4>=Omega(lambda,C_2)>Omega(lambda,4)>23/3. For the last inequality,\nthe primitive root polynomial at 23/3 is\n(253-105lambda)/9<0, since lambda>1207/500>253/105.\nThe bound lambda>1207/500 follows from 707^2=499849<500000=2*500^2.\n\nThe original dominant split (1,0) gives B_2>=G(lambda,1)>379/50.\nFor an explicit radical check at lambda=1207/500,\nG=666/125+sqrt(2539/125)/2. The required root comparison is\nsqrt(2539/125)>563/125, whose squared positive integer difference is\n2539*125-563^2=317375-316969=406>0.\n\nThe ORIGINAL split (4,2) at horizon 7 now gives\n\n B_7>=G(C_4,B_2)>G(23/3,379/50)>611/20.                    (26)\n\nThe final radical has square 3917723/7500. To prove the last inequality\nit is enough to compare that root with 1714/75; after multiplying by\n22500, the squared difference is\n3*3917723-4*2937796=11753169-11751184=1985>0.\nAll compared root thresholds are positive.\n\nOn the other side, lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)<204/35 because\nsqrt(2)<99/70, and gamma=3+sqrt(5)<131/25 because sqrt(5)<56/25.\nBoth follow by squaring positive rational bounds. Therefore\n\n gamma lambda^2 <26724/875,\n 611/20-26724/875=29/3500>0,\n B_7/lambda^2>gamma+29/(3500lambda^2)>gamma+eta,             (27)\n\nusing lambda^2<6. This deliberately coarse positive slack is exact.\nFor m>=2, apply accepted 042's unconditional B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n\nsymbolically m-2 times to the index 7, then monotonicity at the actual\nphysical N_C=2^(m+1):\n\n B_(N_C)>=B_(2^(m+1)-1)>=lambda^(m-2)B_7.\n\nDivision by C=lambda^m proves (25). This preserves all benchmark\nsplits; it never assumes that the dominant maximizing split is balanced.\nEquivalently the unconditional ray has Z_2=lambda Z_1>gamma+eta.\n\nIn particular the explicit member (22) already has\nW/U>(gamma+eta)/gamma>1+eta/6 for every m>=2, by its actual unit\nquadratic payoff. No full improving certificate can exist for it.\n\n### 6.3 Uniform exclusion of EVERY word-dependent member of layout C\n\nThe following argument is not limited to fixed a,b, to bounded guesses,\nor to sublinear assumed tails. Set\n\n r=gamma+eta/2,\n C_cut=(16/eta)^2,\n M_C=max(2,ceil(log C_cut/log lambda)).                      (28)\n\nFor EVERY m>=M_C and EVERY a_m,b_m>=0, the candidate joint conclusion is\n\n W_(N_C)(H_C)>1/(r C),\n W_(N_C)(H_C)/U_(N_C)^ZJ>1+eta/12=1+29/252000.              (29)\n\nProof using the COMPLETE necessary system (23): suppose instead that\nW<=1/(r C). The first two terms in (23) imply\n\n r<=4+2a+(2b-1)/C,\n r a^2<=2+(2b-1)/C.                                       (30)\n\nThe last term implies (b-1)_+<=(2+a)sqrt(C/r), so also\nb<=1+(2+a)sqrt(C/r), with no division by a and no omission when b<=1.\nConsequently, putting\n\n e_C=1/C+2(2+a)/sqrt(r C),\n\nwe have (2b-1)/C<=e_C. Since C>=C_cut>=16 and r>gamma>5,\n\n r a^2<=2+e_C<41/16+a/4.\n\nIf a>=1 the right side is at most (45/16)a^2<5a^2<r a^2,\na contradiction. Thus a<1. It follows that\n\n 0<e_C<1/C+6/sqrt(r C)<=4/sqrt C<=eta/4.                    (31)\n\nFrom the second line of (30),\n\n a<=sqrt((2+e_C)/r)\n   <=sqrt((2+e_C)/gamma)\n   =a_g sqrt(1+e_C/2)<=a_g(1+e_C/4).\n\nHere sqrt(1+t)<=1+t/2 for t>=0 follows by squaring, and\na_g^2 gamma=2 was checked in Section 6. Combining with the first\nline of (30) gives\n\n r<=4+2a+e_C\n   <=gamma+(1+a_g/2)e_C\n   <gamma+3eta/8<gamma+eta/2=r,\n\nagain a contradiction. This proves the first assertion in (29).\nMultiplying by the correct original B_(N_C), using (25), gives\nW/U>(gamma+eta)/(gamma+eta/2)>1+eta/12 since r<6.\nThis proves the second assertion in (29). They are awareness only. Every\nnew geometric and terminal calculation used here is rederived below.\nIn particular, no unverified shared three-cell theorem is a premise.\n\nAll notation in this report is local. Its reusable dictionary is:\nM_mc=M>=2; B_mc=B>=2; A_mc=A>=4B+4; epsilon is in[0,1];\ne_1,e_2,e_3 are the three standard Euclidean basis vectors;\np_mc=p, q_mc=q, r_mc=r are the frozen slopes below;\nQnorm_mc=Q=||q||^2, Rnorm_mc=R=||r||^2;\nC_mc=C=2B+1 is the return-bridge coefficient;\na_mc=a=1-1/M; y_mc=y, z_mc=z and w_mc=w are proximal endpoints;\nt_P,t_Q,t_R,t_D are first-query proximal endpoints of the four stages;\ndelta_P,delta_Q,delta_R,delta_D are their nonnegative support heights.\nH_three,H_return are the two explicitly listed maximum-quadratic laws;\nF_three,F_return are their convex conjugates; V_three,V_return are\ntheir normalized actual terminal payoffs. All local vectors live in R^3.\n\nFreeze the four-plane, full-dimensional dictionary BEFORE the run:\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=e_1/M,\n    q=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,                  Q=2A/M^2,\n    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,         R=BQ/2,\n    <q,r>=-Q/2,\n    J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).\n\nEvery affine offset in J is0 and every maximum weight is1. The three\nnonzero slopes are independent because nu>0 and B/2-1/4>0.\nThus D=conv{0,p,q,r} is a nondegenerate tetrahedron. Define the old\nstatic function explicitly by\n\n    F_old(x)=max_(g in D) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2].                 (1)\n\nIts gradient is the Euclidean projection of x onto D. Projection\noptimality gives firm nonexpansiveness and hence global smoothness1.\nThe minimizing inequalities prove convexity and differentiability.\nThis is also env_1(J): with g=P_D(x), u=x-g, the normal inequality\nmakes g an active subgradient of J(u), and completing the square\nproves that u is its unique proximal minimizer. An interior point of D\nhas gradient x and Hessian I, so the least smoothness is exactly1.\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is {x:<p,x>,<q,x>,<r,x><=0}, value0.\nThe point e_1 has true closest distance1 because p imposes x_1<=0\nand origin attains that distance.\n\nFor every old proximal pair x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u> and\nF_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. For ANY two such pairs, direct expansion gives\n\n    Q_xy=<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0,                               (2)\n\nsince g_x belongs to D and g_y maximizes support at u_y. This pays\nevery prefix, optimum and old terminal row in both orientations.\n\n## 2. Reached chronology common to both constructions\n\nLet each ordinary list be ANY finite nonnegative list of sum M-1.\nUse physical order and retain every individual entry; a list is never\nreplaced by a single oracle step. Initialize at x_0=e_1.\nThe first selected bridge is AM+1, the second is BM+1.\n\nFirst ordinary list and first bridge query: gradient p. At ordinary\npartial mass s in[0,M-1], its proximal point is\n\n    u=(1-(s+1)/M)e_1.\n\nIt has nonnegative p-height, q-height=-2p-height, and r-height=p-height.\nThe latter tie is retained, rather than falsely claimed strict. The\nactive subgradient p makes the proximal point unique, so the actual\nenvelope gradient is p. At the bridge query x=p and u=0. The bridge\nAM+1 sends x to X=-A e_1.\n\nSecond ordinary list and second bridge query: gradient q. Its proximal\npoint is X-tq, 1<=t<=M. The exhaustive other-plane comparisons are\n\n    <q,X-tq>=MQ-tQ=(M-t)Q>=0,\n    <p,X-tq>=(-A+2t/M)/M<0,\n    <r,X-tq>=-<q,X-tq>/2<=0.\n\nThus every actual query is paid, irrespective of individual step size.\nThe ending proximal point is\n\n    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),   <q,y>=<r,y>=0.\n\nThe BM+1 bridge, queried at y+q, sends x to X_R=y-BM q.\n\nThird ordinary list and its following bridge query: gradient r. Its\nproximal point is X_R-tr for 1<=t<=M. Here\n\n    <r,X_R-tr>=(M-t)R>=0,\n    <q,X_R-tr>=-BM Q+tQ/2<0,\n    (X_R-tr)_1=2-A+2B-t/M<0.\n\nThese compare with0,q,p respectively. The exact ending proximal point is\n\n    z=y-BM q-Mr,\n    <r,z>=0,  <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q<0,\n    z_1=1-A+2B<0.                                        (3)\n\nEvery reached update so far is x_next=x-hp, x-hq or x-hr, selected\nby the proved static guards, with h the actual original entry.\nThis is a reached p-to-q-to-r change of directions, not a freely\nspecified switched Hessian word or a pure-generation transport law.\n\n## 3. Screen1: p-to-q-to-r and a completed unqueried terminal\n\nThe full known word is\n\n    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),             (4)\n\nwhere all three L_i have sum M-1. The last retained query is z+r;\nthe terminal point reached from it is x_n=z-M epsilon r.\n\nFirst fix EVERY old preterminal jet from Section2, with its actual old\nvalue. The distinct endpoint anchors are\n\n    t_P=a e_1,                delta_P=a/M,\n    t_Q=y+(M-1)q,             delta_Q=(M-1)Q,\n    t_R=z+(M-1)r,             delta_R=(M-1)R,\n    0, y, z,                 each of height0.\n\nWithin EACH constant-gradient stage the proximal points and heights\ntrace the affine segment between its two endpoints. Therefore its\nconjugate lower quadratics are EXACT convex combinations of the endpoint\nquadratics, including all remote/duplicate queries. The optimum quadratic\nduplicates the0 endpoint. No row is discarded. There are SIX distinct\naffine anchors, rather than the conservative seven in the preliminary plan.\n\nDefine the ONE completed static function BEFORE its actual run:\n\n    H_three(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,\n         <t_P,v>-delta_P,\n         <y,v>, <t_Q,v>-delta_Q,\n         <z,v>, <t_R,v>-delta_R),\n    F_three(x)=sup_v [<x,v>-H_three(v)].                    (5)\n\nThis is the exact full-prefix law of fact1252, not a generic Moreau\nrepresentation. That fact preserves every preceding physical update\nand queried value and gives a finite convex C1 globally1-smooth function.\nWe now derive its actual terminal gradient, without forcing the old r.\n\nPut L=B+1-1/M, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr and\n\n    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2)>=2AB^2,\n    v_0=-M epsilon r,\n    b_3=<E,v_0>-delta_Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),\n    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,\n    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.                                   (6)\n\nHere 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8. If theta_3>0,\nthe z and t_Q terms tie; <z,v_3>=-theta_3<z,E>>0 because\n<z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. If theta_3=0, the z,y,0 terms tie\nat0 and the t_Q term is nonpositive. All remaining anchors are paid:\n\n- <y,v_3>=0 because y is orthogonal to q and r.\n- <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0,\n  so t_R is below z. At epsilon0 its negative offset still applies.\n- <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon<=0, while\n  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0. Thus\n  <t_P-z,v_3><=0<=delta_P.\n\nConsequently z+theta_3 E is an active convex combination of z and t_Q\n(or simply z when theta_3=0), and\n\n    x_n=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).\n\nStrong convexity makes v_3 the unique ACTUAL terminal gradient. The exact\nnormalized value is\n\n    V_three=2F_three(x_n)\n       =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3,\n    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.         (7)\n\nThe old function has terminal value0: its p and q supports are strictly\nnegative and its r support is nonpositive at z-M epsilon r.\nThe gain is a changed unqueried completion, with all earlier values fixed.\n\nThe COMPLETE new minimum set is conv{0,y,z}, value0. Indeed H_three(0)=0\nand its active slopes there are exactly0,y,z since all three deltas are\npositive. Fenchel optimality gives F_three(x)=0 iff x is in\npartial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z}. All its points have first coordinate<=0.\nOrigin is the unique nearest point to e_1, so the TRUE radius stays1.\nFor sufficiently small positive v=t e_1 all nonzero zero-height slopes\nare strictly negative and positive-offset terms are below0. Thus H_three\nis ||v||^2/2 on a nonempty open set; its conjugate has an open Hessian-I\nregion. Advertised smoothness1 is SHARP, with no normalization loss.\n\n## 4. Screen2: an ACTUAL return p-to-q-to-r-to-q\n\nFreeze C=2B+1 and now use the full known word\n\n    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,\n              1+M epsilon).                              (8)\n\nEvery L_i has mass M-1. Assume also M epsilon>=2B^2. At the third\nbridge query the gradient is r and the position is z+r. The CM+1\nbridge sends it to X_D=z-CM r. A fourth ordinary query has proposed\nproximal point X_D-tq, 1<=t<=M. Direct ORIGINAL guards give\n\n    <q,X_D-tq>=(M-t)Q>=0,\n    <r,X_D-tq>=-CM R+tQ/2<0,\n    (X_D-tq)_1=-A+2t/M<0.\n\nThus it really returns to the earlier q face on the SAME fixed function,\nand the return persists through every physical fourth-block query.\nIts ending proximal point is\n\n    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq,\n    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB)<0,\n    w_1=2-A<0.                                          (9)\n\nThe last physical update reaches x_n=w-M epsilon q. Both its old q\nand r supports are negative, and its first coordinate 2-A+2epsilon<0,\nso F_old(x_n)=0. Early energy alone supplies no terminal witness here.\n\nRetain EVERY p,q,r,q prefix jet. In addition to Section3's six anchors,\nretain t_D=w+(M-1)q with delta_D=(M-1)Q, and w with height0.\nThere are exactly EIGHT distinct affine anchors after optimum duplication.\nDefine H_return by adding these two affine terms to H_three, and\nF_return=H_return^*. This exact finite law is fixed before the run.\nThe accepted1252 preserves the actual chronology in Sections2 and4.\n\nThe endpoint-only guess using w and z is WRONG: the FIRST r query t_R\ncan become the active old anchor. The repaired exact calculation follows.\nPut\n\n    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,\n    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,\n    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,\n    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,\n    v_0=-M epsilon q,\n    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-delta_R\n       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],\n    theta_R=b_R/D_R,\n    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.                             (10)\n\nOur assumptions imply b_R>0 and\n\n    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,\n    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<1.           (11)\n\nFor the lower Dhat bound use d>=2B+1 and the increasing quadratic\n1-d+Bd^2/2: at2B+1 it is2B^3+2B^2-3B/2-1>=2B^3.\nFor the upper use d<=2B+2<=3B. The numerator in theta_R is between\nB epsilon/2 and B epsilon because M epsilon>=2B^2 implies\nB M epsilon>=epsilon+B. These justify every inequality in(11).\n\nAll eight affine terms at v_R are now checked:\n\n- The w and t_R terms tie by definition. The w term is positive since\n  <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.\n- The old endpoint z is BELOW w. In fact\n\n      <r,v_R>/(Q/2)\n        =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R>=B.\n\n  The last inequality follows from M epsilon>=2B^2,\n  Dhat_R<=Bd^2/2, d<=3B and B-1/2>=3B/4:\n  2B^2 d(B-1/2)>=B^2 d^2/2. Thus <r,v_R>>=R.\n  As t_R-z=(M-1)r and delta_R=(M-1)R,\n  <z-w,v_R>=(M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<=0.\n- <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0.\n  Hence t_D is below w, and t_Q has negative affine value.\n- The y and0 terms have value0, since y is orthogonal to q,r.\n- For the initial p anchor, set E_P=t_P-w. Then\n  <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon and\n  <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+Ad(CB-1)>=4AB^3.\n  Therefore(11) gives\n  <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0<=delta_P,\n  using A>=4B+4 and B>=2.\n\nNo remaining remote anchor is assumed inactive without a check.\nThe convex combination w+theta_R E_R of the TWO active slopes\nw,t_R gives x_n=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).\nThus v_R is the ACTUAL new terminal gradient, and\n\n    V_return=2F_return(x_n)\n       =2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,\n    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.        (12)\n\nIn particular V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2. The upper follows also from\nthe forced proximal minimizer w and smoothness. The retained first r\nanchor causes a paid, vanishing relative loss; it is not discarded.\n\nThe COMPLETE new minimum set is conv{0,y,z,w}, value0, by the exact\nactive slopes at v=0. Every nonzero listed vertex has negative first\ncoordinate. Thus origin is the unique closest initial minimizer,\nTRUE radius1. The same small-positive-e_1 argument as in Section3\nproves SHARP global smoothness1. Every actual directed prefix, terminal\nand optimum residual, both orientations, holds because this is one actual\nconvex1-smooth function. The formal Gram comes from actual R^3 vectors.\n\n## 5. A concrete failed deletion of the old r-start anchor\n\nThis is a symbolic counterexample to a shortcut, not a third construction.\nTake M=1024,B=2,A=12,epsilon=1/2, C=5. All preceding cap guards and\nM epsilon>=2B^2 hold. If one retains only the minimum anchors w,z at\nthe terminal, the proposed gradient is\n\n    v_bad=-M epsilon q-theta_bad(z-w),\n    theta_bad=(B-1/2)epsilon/[1-C+BC^2/2]=1/28.\n\nIt has <r,v_bad>/R=5M/56=640/7>1. The omitted t_R affine term\nexceeds the z term by (M-1)(<r,v_bad>-R)>0.\nThus the endpoint-only answer is not the maximal-prefix function and\nfails an ACTUAL retained prefix comparison. Section4's eight-anchor\ncalculation repairs this by changing the terminal gradient and value.\nNo deleted incoming/outgoing or optimum rows are inferred from accepted1252.\n\n## 6. Specified growing words beating earlier channels on the SAME words\n\nFor every integer N>=2^60 set\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1,  A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3).\n\nThen B>=2, A=B^10>=4B+4 and M epsilon>=2B^2.\nLet L_up be THREE consecutive increasing copies of Chi_N. Let L_down\nbe THREE consecutive decreasing copies of Chi_N. Both have mass M-1.\nUse L_0=L_up and all subsequent L_i=L_down in(4),(8).\nTheir actual lengths are n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.\nAll caps are reached by the order-independent guards already proved.\nNo mathematical recurrence or trajectory has been executed.\n\nFor either law, its payoff is at least(3/2)M^(-7/12), and in fact\nV_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40). Keep EXACT formulas(7),(12) for the values.\n\nHere is the full K_n screen, including every ordinary and bridge index.\nIn increasing Chi_N, h<=9(1+preceding mass within that copy).\nIn decreasing Chi_N, h<=9(1+following mass within that copy).\nTo justify9, consecutive increasing Chebyshev entries have ratio<=4:\nwrite them as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)), use consecutive angle ratio<=2\nand concavity of sin on[0,pi/2]. The smallest entry is<=4/3.\nAlso max Chi_N=csc^2(pi/(2N+1))<M, by sin t>=2t/pi.\nRepeated copies preserve these within-copy inequalities.\n\n- Every ordinary cut in L_0 has its squared prefix ratio<=81 and\n  tail mass>=AM, so its term is<=41/(AM).\n- Every later ordinary cut has preceding mass>=AM. Write T for its\n  full following mass. The decreasing order gives h<=9(1+T), while\n  h<=M. Hence h^2/(1+2T)<=9M and its term is<=9/(A^2 M).\n- The AM+1 bridge term is<=A^2/(2BM).\n- The BM+1 bridge term is<=B^2/(A^2 M).\n- In the returning word, the CM+1 bridge term is<=C^2/(A^2 M)\n  <=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M).\n- The final bridge term is<=epsilon^2/A^2.\n- The global Huber term is<=1/(2AM).\n\nSubstitution of the exact powers of M gives for BOTH complete words\n\n    K_n(h)<=41 M^(-5/6).                                (13)\n\nThe reverse order in later lists is load-bearing. An increasing FINAL\nlist would invalidate this small-K argument near its last large entry;\nthat failed order-free shortcut is not used.\n\nAll convex quadratic witnesses in any dimension reduce by orthogonal\ndiagonalization to Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product(1-h_j t)^2.\nWrite T=(2N+1)^2=2M-1. The accepted Chebyshev identity gives\n|P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(Tt)). The first bound follows from\n|sin((2N+1)theta)|<=(2N+1)|sin theta| by telescoping unit complex\nnumbers; the second from |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1.\nSetting u=Tt, the complete three-cap quadratic expression is bounded by\n\n    T^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),\n\nand the returning expression has an additional (1+Cu)^2 and u^(-12)\nin place of u^(-9). Split u<=1 and u>=1 and bound each positive\ncoefficient factor by twice its coefficient times max(1,u). This gives\n\n    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T<=64 M^(-49/60),\n    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T<=1600 M^(-4/5).      (14)\n\nThe actual bridge/T ratios are <=A,B,C,1 respectively; no physical\nstep was changed in this comparison. The order of Chebyshev copies\ndoes not alter their polynomial.\n\nThe universal-lens channel can be bounded on the same words. Since\nn>=N, Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. For Kc=cosh(2), the accepted\nellipse estimate and, when T|z|<=1, the direct product bound\n|P_N(z)|<=exp(s(Chi_N)|z|)<=exp(1/6)<=Kc, give\n|P_N(z)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T|z|)). The same calculation as(14),\nnow using Gap_h(z)<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2, yields\n\n    sup_Lens_n Gap_h_three <=128 Kc^18 M^(-49/60),\n    sup_Lens_n Gap_h_return<=3200 Kc^24 M^(-4/5).         (15)\n\nSince p=log_2(3)<8/5 (3^5<2^8), M>=2N^2 and n<=16N, accepted\nB_n<=1+4n^p and(13)-(15) prove\n\n    max(K_n,Q_n,sup_Lens_n Gap_h)=o(U_n^ZJ)              (16)\n\nfor each of these TWO specified full word families. This asserts nothing\nabout the full schedule-dependent complex legal region.\n\nFor the attained witness lower comparison, use the EXACT all-split\nrecurrences, not an assumed profile constant. They imply C_n>=C_(n-1),\nC_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt2 by the balanced split,\nand B_n>=2C_(n-1)-1. If d_N=floor(log_2 N), then n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1,\nso B_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Also M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Therefore\n\n    B_n V >=(3lambda^3/[2*20^(7/12)])\n                     (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1,        (17)\n\nfor both families at every N>=2^60. The exact elementary inequalities\nlambda>12/5, 2^(7/6)<9/4, 20^(7/12)<8 make the prefactor>2 and\nthe base>16/15. Thus the ratio in(17) diverges.\n\nTo cover EVERY sufficiently large integer horizon for each family,\ntake N=floor((n-3)/9) or floor((n-4)/12) respectively and insert the\nremaining0through8 or0through11 zero steps at the VERY BEGINNING.\nThey repeat the initialized jet, retain all original rows and leave\nthe unqueried terminal free. Use exactly the same static H,F law.\nIts true radius and terminal value are unchanged. The bounds use the\nactual padded n and the actual all-split B_n. Thus both are attained\nall-large-horizon SPECIAL-FAMILY lower witnesses exceeding U_n^ZJ.\n\n## 7. What these policies add to the earlier two-block example\n\nThese paths have three or four physically queried gradient stages;\nScreen2 actually returns to a retained earlier face. All normal components,\nstage heights and intercepts are fixed, with a radius-one initial point.\nThere is no pure-generation mass dilution, imposed matrix word, uniform\npositive lower Hessian window, or one-cap/one-cap single-cut continuation.\n\nA precise same-word comparison with the NATURAL collapsed two-block\npolicy from shared e2dad is available, without adopting that draft as a\npremise. Keep p=e_1/M and the bridge AM+1. Regard all remaining steps\nBEFORE the final bridge as one ordinary block of mass M_2-1. For(4),\nM_2=(B+2)M; for(8), M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M. The frozen second slope\nq_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2 has norm squared2A/M_2^2.\nThe same old-cap guards prove every query legal. Its last proximal point\nis an actual zero-height minimum for EVERY legal prefix completion:\nthe last query has f=||q_2||^2/2, and a unit step gives f(y_2)<=0.\nConsequently every completion of THIS complete prefix has normalized\nterminal value<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2, by smoothness about y_2.\nThe initial p jet forces true closest radius1 for every such completion.\n\nOur three-stage value exceeds that upper by a factor at least\nB(B+2)^2(1-1/(2B^3))/2. Our returning value exceeds the corresponding\nupper by at least(3/4)(3B+4)^2. Both diverge. This comparison covers\nthe specifically defined collapsed policy, not EVERY possible two-block\npartition, arbitrary second slope, scalar function, or spiral witness.\nThe broader established witness portfolio is not upper-bounded here.\n\n## 8. Precise unpaid bridge and a diagnostic scoped failure\n\nThe sufficient arbitrary-block contracts proved here are literal:\nequal incoming masses M-1, first bridge excess AM, second excess BM,\nA>=4B+4, B>=2; the returning policy requires excess CM with C=2B+1\nand M epsilon>=2B^2 at the last bridge. No claim is made that an arbitrary\nword admits this partition, these ratios or a paid continuation afterward.\n\nOn the assigned inherited-(m,tau=1/4) hierarchy, the naive first-occurrence\ninstantiation has first prefix H_j, M=1+S_j and A=(b_j-1)/M<1.\nFor m>=7 its enclosing c<3 and a/(c-2)<1, so the symbolic sum\nM=v c^j+(1-v)2^j implies A<c-2<1. This contradicts A>=4B+4.\nFor j=0, A=a<1. Therefore this SPECIFIC saturated three-cap policy cannot\nbe licensed at those first-occurrence hierarchy cuts. This is a failure\nof its sufficient geometry, not an upper on W or on unscreened policies.\n\nThe main theorem still needs a different all-word decomposition or a\nmultistage law valid at much smaller intermediate bridge ratios, while\nretaining an original terminal objective and a word-independent\nconstant-profile margin. That missing uniform statement is a theorem-level\ngap. The special-family gains in(17) do not close it.\n\n## 9. Retrieval, audit and allocation discipline\n\nSkills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and\nconstruct-counterexamples. Fact search was performed before new proving.\nShared scalar multiscale, fresh-child tree/generation, projection-polytope,\nand free-terminal scope failures were recalled.
